# Skills · C · part 76

853 entries, chromatographic-error-window-coverage-calculation to chrome-mcp-then-markdown. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **chromatographic-error-window-coverage-calculation**: Use when you have trained retention-time prediction models (e.g. Retip's fit.rf, fit.keras, fit.xgboost, fit.lightgbm, fit.brnn) and experimental RT values for test/validation compounds, and need to quantify what fraction of true positives fall within a fixed RT error window. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-error-window-coverage-calculation))
- **chromatographic-feature-correlation-clustering**: Use when input is MS1-only mzML/mzXML (or imzML) data lacking tandem spectra and the goal is to assemble in-source fragments correlated with a precursor into pseudo MS/MS spectra for structural annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-correlation-clustering))
- **chromatographic-feature-correlation-filtering**: Use when you have a chromatographic peak table from LC-MS lipidomics (e.g. Compound Discoverer output) with identified lipid features and numerous unidentified features, and you want to filter out co-eluting spurious/redundant features before downstream statistics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-correlation-filtering))
- **chromatographic-feature-extraction**: Use when you have centroided mzML files from LC- or GC-HRMS instruments (acquired in data-dependent mode with ddMS2) and need to systematically identify chromatographic peaks, measure their mass and retention time coordinates, and quantify their intensities before applying PFAS-specific. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-feature-extraction) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-feature-matching**: Use when two peak-detection tools have been run on the same LC-HRMS samples and you must match their detected features, compare peak areas, and quantify correlation (R2) and systematic area-scaling factors. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-matching))
- **chromatographic-feature-metadata-extraction**: Reference for extracting per-feature metadata such as peak_area and calibration/IS metavalues from FeatureDB reports in targeted metabolomics workflows before fitting calibration curves or computing quantitation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-metadata-extraction))
- **chromatographic-feature-refinement**: Use when after performing retention-time-based grouping on LC-MS data (e.g., with a 10–20 second window) and observing that many feature groups contain features with similar retention times but dissimilar abundance patterns across samples or different extracted ion chromatogram peak shapes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-feature-refinement) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-feature-table-construction**: Use when starting from raw LC-MS/MS MS1 scans (mzML or Bruker .d) and a feature table with complete signal coverage is needed before adduct grouping, alignment, or annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-table-construction))
- **chromatographic-fwhm-overlap-scoring**: Use when assembling LC-MS mass traces into metabolite isotope features and you must verify that candidate isotope traces co-elute with the monoisotopic trace before accepting a feature hypothesis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-fwhm-overlap-scoring))
- **chromatographic-gradient-evaluation**: Use when raw LC-MS (mzML/mzXML) data from a gradient run must be reduced to a single separation-performance number (GSI) to evaluate or optimize a chromatographic gradient. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-gradient-evaluation))
- **chromatographic-gradient-parameterization**: Use when you must define the candidate LC-MS gradient space before fitting the GP model: you have gradient-definition parameters (dimensionality, time points, mobile-phase settings) and need the constrained gridX/scaledX candidate set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-gradient-parameterization))
- **chromatographic-ion-clusterisation**: Use when analysing HPTLC coupled with high-resolution mass spectrometry data (e.g. mzXML-derived extracted ion chromatograms) and you need to partition ions of interest from background ions before structure elucidation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-ion-clusterisation))
- **chromatographic-local-maxima-detection**: Use when processing raw profile-mode LC-HRMS chromatograms (mzXML/mzML) and you need to enumerate all candidate peak-apex signals before machine-learning peak/background classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-local-maxima-detection))
- **chromatographic-method-transfer**: Use when you have retention time predictions from a source chromatographic method and need to predict retention times for a target chromatographic method, but have limited calibration data (10–100 molecules) measured on both methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v1/skills/chromatographic-method-transfer) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-method-transfer-learning**: Use when you have experimental RT measurements from a source chromatographic method and need to predict RTs for the same molecules on a target chromatographic method, but lack a large calibration dataset (typical scenario: 10–100 molecules with ground truth RTs on both methods). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-method-transfer-learning) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-misalignment-detection**: Use when after running XCMS-based alignment on LC-MS datasets with hundreds of samples or data acquisition periods longer than a week, when the assumption that all m/z bins in the same sample share a single warping function is likely to fail. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-misalignment-detection) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-modality-classification**: Use when when ingesting raw or vendor-format mass spectrometry data files of unknown or mixed acquisition modality, and you need to automatically determine whether the input originated from liquid chromatography (LC), gas chromatography (GC), ion mobility spectrometry (IMS), or MS imaging (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-modality-classification) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-model-peak-selection**: Use when SWATH-MS data have multiplexed MS/MS spectra in which fragment ions co-elute and chromatographic peaks are unresolved, and metabolite-specific MS2 spectra must be reconstructed for downstream annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-model-peak-selection))
- **chromatographic-modulation-folding**: Use when raw comprehensive two-dimensional GC–MS data in CDF files must be folded into a 4D tensor (samples, m/z fragments, second-dimension and first-dimension retention time) as the entry point of an untargeted GC×GC–MS workflow. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-modulation-folding))
- **chromatographic-noise-modeling**: Use when you need to generate blank or background-only .mzML files for method validation, when you want to create synthetic negative controls with realistic instrumental noise but no analyte peaks, or when you need to simulate serum matrix background (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-noise-modeling) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-alignment-input-preparation**: Use when raw LC/HRMS data (mzXML, mzML, netCDF) must be converted into chromatographic m/z-RT peaklists and an aligned peak table before running the CSA, DDA, or DIA deconvolution workflows of IDSL.CSA. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-alignment-input-preparation))
- **chromatographic-peak-alignment-qc**: Use when GC/MS chromatograms have already been deconvolved with eRah and aligned compound tables contain gaps (compounds found in some samples but not others) that must be filled before quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-alignment-qc))
- **chromatographic-peak-annotation-visualization**: Use when after running tardisPeaks() with screening_mode=TRUE on centroided .mzML LC-MS data, when you need to visually inspect whether the 10 target compounds (internal standards and endogenous metabolites) were correctly detected within their expected m/z and retention time windows. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-annotation-visualization) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-area-integration**: Use when processing targeted MS data where peak detection has produced EICs (after airPLS baseline correction and MassSpecWavelet peak positioning) and per-sample peak areas must be computed for a chosen isotopologue or metabolite. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-area-integration))
- **chromatographic-peak-baseline-reconstruction**: Use when MRM ion chromatograms contain noisy peaks, unevenly sampled signals, or coeluted isomers where connecting nearby valleys (e.g., TargetLynx) misestimates the baseline; adaptive section-wise background reconstruction is needed before peak integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-baseline-reconstruction))
- **chromatographic-peak-boundary-correction**: Use when refining the start/stop limits of peaks detected in smoothed CE-MS or HILIC-MS ROI chromatograms (intensity-vs-retention-time traces), after CWT/Mexican-hat peak-center detection but before peak integration of RT, area, and height. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-boundary-correction))
- **chromatographic-peak-boundary-detection**: Use when reconstructing chromatographic peak boundaries from centroided LC-MS data — e.g. when a growing mass trace spans several valleys and must be split so each trace corresponds to one chromatographic peak, or when apex-based boundary definition is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-boundary-detection))
- **chromatographic-peak-characterization**: Use when after screening-mode validation of your m/z and retention time (RT) windows has confirmed that targets are visible in your data. Use it when you have centroided .mzML files, a validated target compound table with adjusted expected RT values (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-characterization) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-classification**: Use when you have (1) a benchmark dataset of reference peaks with validated m/z, retention time boundaries, and isotopologue assignments, and (2) NPP output feature tables (unaligned and aligned) from tools like XCMS, MZmine 2, or MS-DIAL that you wish to evaluate. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-classification) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection**: Use when raw LC-MS targeted metabolomics EICs (e.g. centWave-exported chromatograms such as the MM14 or human blood datasets) need peak positions, bounds and areas quantified per compound, especially when multiple candidate peaks must be resolved to the true M+H/M+Na/M+K isotopologue peak. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection--5fea644988e8baaa))
- **chromatographic-peak-detection**: Use when MRM-mode LC-QQQ-MS raw data (mzML) must be preprocessed into peak intensity tables without manual review, e.g. batches from instruments like an Agilent 6495 QQQ-MS needing automatic peak picking and quality control. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection--ff4f929bb4f7747e))
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-and-grouping**: Use when when you have processed LC-MS data through XCMS alignment but suspect misaligned features due to retention-time drift over long acquisition periods (&gt;1 week) or large sample batches (hundreds of samples), or when peak-filling produces unexpectedly high coefficient-of-variation (CV &gt; 0. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-and-grouping) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-and-integration**: Use when after loading centroided .mzML LC-MS data and defining a target list (compound ID, name, m/z, RT, polarity) when you need to: (1) automatically locate and integrate peaks for known compounds across multiple runs; (2) generate per-target and per-run quantitative metrics; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-and-integration) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-and-segmentation**: Use when you have raw LC-MS data (mzML or vendor format) and need to discover and characterize all chromatographic features present, without prior knowledge of target analytes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-and-segmentation) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-gradient-descent**: Use when you have LC-HRMS profile-mode data (retention time × m/z matrix format) and need to automatically identify chromatographic peak locations and boundaries prior to feature extraction, reference matching, or training a peak-classification CNN model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-gradient-descent) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-msw**: Use when you have raw mzML files from an FTICR-MS or other direct-injection MS instrument and need to identify discrete chromatographic peaks across the m/z and retention-time dimensions. Use this skill when you must isolate individual ion signals before applying calibration corrections (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-msw) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-retention-time-alignment**: Use when you have multiple high-resolution mzML files from LC-MS/MS experiments (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-retention-time-alignment) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-validation**: Use when you have centroided mzML data and a curated set of target molecules with known retention time (RT) boundaries, and you need to establish ground-truth peak detection performance metrics (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-validation) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-wavelet**: Use when you have loaded raw LC-MS or direct-injection FTICR-MS data (in mzML or netCDF format) into an XCMSnExp object and need to identify individual chromatographic peaks before feature grouping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-wavelet) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-with-prominence-control**: Use when after constructing baseline-corrected mass tracks (either composite across samples or per-sample) when you need to identify individual chromatographic peaks for feature extraction in LC-MS or GC-MS metabolomics workflows. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-with-prominence-control) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-edge-detection**: Use when processing raw LC-MS ROI intensity arrays (e.g. mzML or Bruker TDF2 .d via MassCube) and you need to split each mass trace into discrete chromatographic peak segments bounded by start/end edge indices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-edge-detection))
- **chromatographic-peak-extraction-and-retention-time-filtering**: Use when when you have LC-MS/MS raw data (mzML or netCDF format) and need to isolate a specific compound's signal based on its known or suspected m/z value and retention time range. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-extraction-and-retention-time-filtering) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-fwhm-window-grouping**: Use when an aligned XCMS feature table (m/z, retention time, apex intensity, per-sample intensities) must be partitioned into co-eluting compound spectra as the first CAMERA grouping step before isotope/adduct annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-fwhm-window-grouping))
- **chromatographic-peak-integration-rt-area-height**: Use when untargeted metabolomics ROIs (intensity-vs-retention-time traces from CE-MS or HILIC-MS runs, e.g. .mzML/.mzXML converted profiles) must be converted into a per-peak table of apex RT, area, and height for feature integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-integration-rt-area-height))
- **chromatographic-peak-isolation-and-refinement**: Use when after sliding-window analysis has identified candidate TIC peaks but before extracting chromatographic parameters (retention time, peak width, intensity) from extracted ion chromatograms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-isolation-and-refinement) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-labeling**: Use when you have a user-defined reference list of isolated, high-confidence chromatographic peaks (ground-truth) matched across multiple LC-HRMS samples, and you need to produce a labelled training dataset large enough to train a CNN model that discriminates true peaks from background noise and. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-labeling) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-ms2-spectra-extraction**: Use when you have an xcms result object with centWave-detected chromatographic peaks (e.g. from an mzML LC-MS/MS run like HighIS_Mix07_CE20_POS.mzML) and need the tandem MS2 spectra behind those peaks to compare against a reference library such as an HMDB 5.0 CompDb. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-ms2-spectra-extraction))
- **chromatographic-peak-overlap-resolution**: Use when analyzing GC-MS data containing overlapping chromatographic peaks—a common scenario in untargeted metabolomics and environmental screening where sample complexity or chromatographic resolution limitations cause co-elution of structurally similar or temporally proximate compounds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-overlap-resolution) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-picking**: Use when starting from raw HRMS .mzML files and you need to extract chromatographic features (m/z–RT pairs) for non-targeted analysis, e.g. ENTACT-style mixtures or environmental water samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-picking--2cc39905d492bb0b))
- **chromatographic-peak-picking**: Use when starting from raw MS data files (e.g. LC-MS runs) and a feature-level peak list is needed as the basis for deisotoping, alignment, and gap filling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-picking--84ee8dab25694863))
- **chromatographic-peak-picking**: Use when raw LC-MS mzXML files (any vendor format compatible with MS-Convert) need MS1 peak picking to build a JPA feature table, or as the PP baseline step before MR and targeted feature extraction in metabolomics/exposomics processing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-picking--c371cbed79b4941f))
- **chromatographic-peak-processing**: Use when after peak detection when you have a table of detected peaks with m/z values and retention times from LC/HRMS data, and you observe systematic m/z drift across a batch or population-scale study (n &gt; 500 samples). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-processing) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment**: Use when you have targeted/suspect HRMS data (mzML) plus a compound or mass list and need per-peak QC (MS1/MS2 existence, noise, RT shift) before further identification or spectral upload. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment--9af6849a84c39e4f))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment**: Use when when you have a feature table (CSV with m/z and retention time columns) and corresponding LC-MS raw data files (.mzXML or .mzML) from an untargeted metabolomics experiment, and need to filter out spurious peaks before downstream statistical or biological interpretation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment**: Use when extracted ion chromatograms (EICs) from LC/MS metabolomics profiles have already been peak-detected and individual peaks (or EICs) must be scored, filtered, or classified as good vs. bad before downstream processing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment--577231b7ebfad399))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering**: Use when raw centroid/profile HRMS scans (.mzML) have been peak-picked and the feature list must be cleaned of low-quality peaks — split peaks, peaks spanning too few scans, and high-baseline-noise features — before annotation or statistical analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering--e10fb7f7afcb7891))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering**: Use when processing LC-MS mzML files of authentic metabolite standards and you need to find peaks on adduct EICs and remove low-quality detections before assigning retention times and m/z for an in-house reference library. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering--513a3253f15d053d))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering**: Use when processing MS1-level LC/HRMS peak candidates (e.g. from IDSL.IPA EIC detection) and you need to discard low-quality or redundant isotope peaks using the seven-parameter peak quality filter before building aligned data matrices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering--7ffa5de193723c9b))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation**: Use when you have an XCMS pre-processed untargeted LC–MS dataset (getEIC + fillPeaks output) and need numeric per-peak quality features to train or apply a Pass/Fail peak-quality classifier. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation--8df5c515366f3394))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation**: Use when LC/MS chromatographic peaks have been detected on extracted ion chromatograms (EICs) and you need per-peak numeric quality scores to filter 'bad' peaks before downstream metabolomics processing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation--506f40a4b68c44fd))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-evaluation**: Use when when processing untargeted LC-MS metabolomics data with XCMS and need to identify low-quality peak integrations that may introduce noise or bias into subsequent compound identification and quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-evaluation) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metrics**: Use when evaluating LC-MS metabolomics peak quality for chemical standards: you have EICs from mzXML/mzML files (with optional replicate and blank runs) and need standardized per-peak quality metrics to validate or rank peaks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metrics))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-score-computation**: Use when preprocessing LC-QQQ-MS raw data in MRM mode and you need per-candidate peak quality metrics (shape, quantifier/qualifier coelution, RT deviation) before ML-based peak reporting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-score-computation))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-scoring**: Use when raw MRM-mode LC-MS data (mzML with quantifier plus qualifier transitions and expected RTs in metabdb.xlsx) must be converted into a trustworthy set of reported peaks, i.e. deciding which of several peak candidates per metabolite/sample is the correct one. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-scoring))
- **chromatographic-peak-r13c-computation**: Reference for quantifying the 12C/13C isotope-pair intensity ratio of individual chromatographic peaks and correlating it with molecular formulas or predicted carbon number. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-r13c-computation))
- **chromatographic-peak-resolution**: Use when when you have loaded raw GC-MS data in netCDF or mzML format and visual or statistical inspection reveals overlapping chromatographic peaks (i.e., multiple m/z ions co-eluting at the same retention time window). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-resolution) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-accuracy-benchmarking**: Use when a peak-detection algorithm needs quantitative accuracy assessment against ground-truth manually labeled EIC peak-shape classes (double vs single peak) on experimental LC-MS data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-accuracy-benchmarking))
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-analysis**: Use when raw CE-MS or LC-MS data (converted to .mzXML/.mzML via msconvert) has been peak-detected with the CWT/ROI search step and noise/false-positive peaks must be culled by shape before feature integration, scaling, and mid-level data fusion. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-analysis))
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-comparison**: Use when after retention-time-based and abundance-correlation-based feature grouping have produced composite feature groups, and you need to identify which features within a group actually represent different compounds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-comparison) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation**: Use when processing untargeted LC-MS raw data (mzML or Bruker .d) where co-eluting ions must be distinguished as genuine metabolites versus in-source fragments or alternative adducts before quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation))
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation-analysis**: Decision support for splitting CAMERA pseudospectra that the retention-time window alone left ambiguous — i.e. when features co-elute within one FWHM group but their EIC peak shapes (corval, pval thresholds on calcCaS edges) indicate two overlapping analytes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation-analysis))
- **chromatographic-peak-smoothing-and-noise-estimation**: Use when performing semi-targeted LC-MS peak quantification on converted Aird data (e.g. TripleTOF or QE HF acquisitions) and a smoothing or noise-estimation strategy must be selected among MetaPro's 4 smoothing and 3 noise-estimation options before peak selection and integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-smoothing-and-noise-estimation))
- **chromatographic-peak-snr-filtering**: Use when filtering candidate peaks detected from GC×GC–MS 4D intensity arrays (CDF-derived GcDuo objects) by signal-to-noise and peak-shape criteria before deconvolution, to discard false positives such as incomplete or sub-threshold signals. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-snr-filtering))
- **chromatographic-peak-tip-detection**: Use when processing high-throughput LC-MS/MS MRM ion chromatograms where peaks may be coeluted, noisy, or unevenly sampled and per-transition peak tips, valleys, and coelution flags must be resolved before quantitation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-tip-detection))
- **chromatographic-peak-tracking-for-user-defined-compounds**: Use when you have raw untargeted metabolomics files (mzML/mzXML/mzData) plus a user-defined list of m/z and RT pairs for QC or contaminant compounds, and need to track observed m/z, retention time, intensity, and peak shape over time or batches. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-tracking-for-user-defined-compounds))
- **chromatographic-peak-validation**: Use when replicate LC/MS injections of chemical standard mixtures (mzXML/mzML with blank runs) must be filtered into validated peaks to build a retention-time reference library, e.g. the HILIC LC-MS platform's 571-metabolite library. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-validation))
- **chromatographic-peak-vs-background-classification**: Use when you have profile-mode LC-HRMS chromatograms and a user-defined reference-feature list (ground truth) and need to distinguish true chromatographic peaks from background/walls with a custom-trained CNN. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-vs-background-classification))
- **chromatographic-peak-width-measurement**: Use when you need to set chromatographic peak-width parameters for an LC-MS feature extraction run (e.g. XCMS CentWave peak width, MS-DIAL tolerances) from raw untargeted metabolomics data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-width-measurement))
- **chromatographic-peak-width-tuning**: Use when configuring eRah deconvolveComp() on GC-EI MS chromatograms (netCDF/mzXML) and you must set min.peak.width, min.peak.height, noise.threshold, or avoid.processing.mz before running CMLC/OSD. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-width-tuning))
- **chromatographic-profile-quality-assessment**: Use when running targeted peak detection on LC-MS data acquired with multiple overlapping m/z scan windows and observing distorted or periodically discontinuous peak profiles in EIC plots. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-profile-quality-assessment) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-quality-metric-calculation**: Use when targeted LC–MS peak integration (e.g., TARDIS on raw metabolomics/lipidomics runs) has produced per-target peak tables and you need quantitative quality metrics to filter poor-quality peaks or validate QC-run clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-quality-metric-calculation))
- **chromatographic-quality-metric-implementation**: Use when you must programmatically score the quality of extracted ion chromatograms (EICs) or detected chromatographic peaks in LC/MS metabolomics data — especially EICs with high background signals or containing only a single peak — before downstream peak detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-quality-metric-implementation))
- **chromatographic-quantitation-curve-modeling**: Use when absolute quantitation of targeted compounds from MRM data is needed and calibration standard samples (e.g. 14 standards spanning 0.025–500 mol/mL) are available to build concentration–abundance curves. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-quantitation-curve-modeling))
- **chromatographic-resolution-evaluation**: Use when when you have a feature table from LC-MS peak detection (e.g., output from MassCube's nontargeted peak segmentation step) and need to assess which features have adequate chromatographic separation from coeluting or nearby peaks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-resolution-evaluation) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-retention-matching-window-analysis**: Use when a predicted-versus-true retention time table exists and you need to set an RT matching window (e.g., ±2 SD) for metabolite identification and quantify how many true matches that window would exclude. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-retention-matching-window-analysis))
- **chromatographic-retention-order-prediction**: Use when the goal is to predict elution order of metabolites directly from molecular structure (InChI) across LC systems, especially when retention times are not comparable across instruments/configurations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-retention-order-prediction))
- **chromatographic-retention-time-correction**: Use when isothermal capillary-GC retention times (e.g. 40-120°C in 20°C increments on SE-30) must be dead-time corrected, replicate-averaged, and regressed against 1/T to derive enthalpies/entropies of vaporization. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/chromatographic-retention-time-correction))
- **chromatographic-retention-time-error-tracking**: Use when raw LC-MS or LC-IMS-MS files (Agilent 'd', Thermo '.raw', Bruker 'd', mzML) need per-ion mass, retention-time, and arrival-time error metrics for QC of chromatographic performance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-retention-time-error-tracking))
- **chromatographic-retention-time-matching**: Use when you have GC-MS data with multiple replicate injections or samples, need to identify a predefined set of query chemicals by name, and want to consolidate all instances of those chemicals (which may appear with varying match factors or peak areas across different samples or injection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-retention-time-matching) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-retention-time-replicate-averaging**: Use when capillary GC retention times for reference/relative vapour pressure determination (e.g. tetraorganostannanes on an SE-30 column, 40–120°C) are collected as multiple replicate runs and must be averaged and hold-up corrected. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/chromatographic-retention-time-replicate-averaging))
- **chromatographic-retention-time-reproducibility-analysis**: Use when replicate LC-MS/MS runs of histone tail separations are available and the goal is to quantify run-to-run retention-time variation per histone tail class (H3, H4, H2A) against explicit minute-level acceptance thresholds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/chromatographic-retention-time-reproducibility-analysis))
- **chromatographic-rt-dataset-preparation**: Use when you need to train or retrain RT-Transformer on a retention time dataset prepared as InChI+RT CSV, or reproduce the SMRT full-data MAE of 33.41 s. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-rt-dataset-preparation))
- **chromatographic-rt-prediction-benchmark-analysis**: Use when comparing retention time prediction models on a benchmark dataset and you need to determine which achieves the lowest mean/median relative error, e.g. GNN-RT (4.9% mean, 3.2% median) vs RF, Bayesian ridge, CNN, DLM. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-rt-prediction-benchmark-analysis))
- **chromatographic-rt-tolerance-configuration**: Use when constructing metScribeR reference libraries from mzML files of authentic standards and you must choose the m/z and RT tolerances for EIC extraction and adduct distinction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-rt-tolerance-configuration))
- **chromatographic-shape-similarity-analysis**: Use when processing MRM acquisition data where EIC peak features across samples must be grouped by chromatographic shape (e.g., same target peptide transition across samples), and manual inspection needs pre-grouped EICs to keep low-intensity noise features separated from real signal. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-shape-similarity-analysis))
- **chromatographic-shift-correction-evaluation**: Use when aligning feature retention times across many LC-MS chromatograms (e.g., large QC-pooled metabolomics studies) and you need quantifiable residual RT-shift benchmarks after correction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-shift-correction-evaluation))
- **chromatographic-signal-preprocessing**: Use when raw GCxGC-MS chromatograms (e.g., NetCDF files folded into 2D-TIC matrices) contain a steady increasing baseline, noise, or retention-time shifts that must be removed prior to MPCA or supervised modeling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-signal-preprocessing))
- **chromatographic-snr-computation-from-eic-flanks**: Use when validating asari feature quality without manual inspection: you have full_Feature_table.tsv plus export/cmap.pickle and must recompute snr, cSelectivity, or peakshape to check exported values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-snr-computation-from-eic-flanks))
- **chromatographic-space-similarity-quantification**: Use when you have paired RPLC retention datasets (e.g., RepoRT, PredRet, Metabolome Workbench, MetaboLights sources) sharing compounds across different chromatographic methods and need to measure retention-order replicability or attribute it to specific method differences. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-space-similarity-quantification))
- **chromatographic-system-train-test-splitting**: Use when evaluating RankSVM or SVR retention order/time prediction across multiple chromatographic systems (e.g. Eawag_XBridgeC18, FEM_long, RIKEN, UFZ_Phenomenex, LIFE_old) and you must decide which systems serve as training vs. target data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-system-train-test-splitting))
- **chromatographic-trace-extraction**: Use when you have a profile-mode mzML file from LC-DTIM-MS and a list of molecular formulae (or predicted ion m/z values) and need to locate each monoisotopic primary-ion m/z trace over RT and arrival time, then peak-pick in the RT dimension. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-trace-extraction))
- **chromatographic-transferability-assessment**: Use when deciding whether fine-tuning a pre-trained ROASMI model with a specific reference dataset will yield substantial rank-score gains on a target prediction set, or when grouping retraining results by ChemSS/ChrSS similarity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-transferability-assessment))
- **chromatographic-window-coverage-assessment**: Use when after extracting retention times from top MS1 features detected in an LC-MS run, and when you need to evaluate whether a given gradient time range (e.g., 0–30 minutes) is being used efficiently to separate compounds. Apply this skill as the objective function in gradient optimization (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-window-coverage-assessment) · 2 copies)
- **chromatography**: HPLC, GC, TLC, column chromatography, separation mechanisms, retention models, and method development ([Mr-Nobody-Anonymous/All-skills](https://github.com/Mr-Nobody-Anonymous/All-skills/tree/HEAD/awesome_skills/chemistry/chromatography))
- **chromatography-data-decimation**: Use when working with large GCIMS matrices where computational speed or memory constraints are a concern, after filtering retention time (e.g., 0–1100 s) and drift time (e.g., 5–16 ms) ranges and applying Savitzky-Golay smoothing. Use it as a preprocessing step before alignment operations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatography-data-decimation) · 3 copies)
- **chromatography-data-preprocessing**: Use when you have raw spectra files from a liquid chromatography experiment (in-house or external database) and need to adapt a pretrained GNN-RT model to predict retention times for your molecules. Preprocessing is the mandatory first step before any model training or transfer learning can proceed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatography-data-preprocessing) · 3 copies)
- **chromatography-mass-spectrometry-data-processing**: Use when when you have raw GC–MS or LC–MS data (m/z vs retention time chromatography-mass spectrometry maps) and need to identify analyte signals and marker features without conventional peak picking; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatography-mass-spectrometry-data-processing) · 2 copies)
- **chromatography-ms-2d-map-visualization**: Use when input is a two-dimensional chromatography–MS map (m/z vs retention time) from GC–MS or LC–MS and you need to discriminate analytes and identify/visualize marker features without conventional peak picking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatography-ms-2d-map-visualization))
- **chromatography-ms-map-image-processing**: Use when raw 2D chromatography–MS maps (m/z vs retention time, GC–MS or LC–MS) need analyte discrimination and marker-feature identification/visualization and conventional peak picking is unreliable or causes false peak detections. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatography-ms-map-image-processing))
- **chromatography-ms-marker-feature-identification**: Use when you have raw two-dimensional chromatography–MS maps (GC–MS or LC–MS, e.g. m/z vs retention time) and need untargeted marker feature identification without prior peak picking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatography-ms-marker-feature-identification))
- **chromatography-quantification**: Quantify analytes from chromatographic data with validated integration, calibration, quality controls, and uncertainty. Use for HPLC, UHPLC, GC, LC-MS, GC-MS, UV or fluorescence detection, targeted assays, purity estimates, retention data, and batch acceptance. ([eightmm/codex-science](https://github.com/eightmm/codex-science/tree/HEAD/authored-skills/chromatography-quantification) · ★4)
- **chrombpnet-skill**: Build, validate, run, and troubleshoot ChromBPNet workflows for bias-factorized base-resolution ATAC-seq or DNase-seq modeling.
